| A published, peer-reviewed accuracy figure for a detection tool is often **one of several** the same paper reports for that same tool, under different accuracy computations — so quoting one without naming the computation reports a settledness the paper refuses. Measured instances: Weber-Wulff et al. (IJEI 19:26, 2023) give ZeroGPT **59%** (Table 7, binary), **74%** (Table 8, binary inclusive) and **67%** (Table 9, semi-binary); Perkins et al. (2024) give ZeroGPT **46.1%** (Table 7, baseline, itself a mean of three) and **31.3%** (Table 8, non-manipulated output) | High (two instances, first-hand) | doi:10.1007/s40979-023-00146-z ; doi:10.1186/s41239-024-00487-w | Session 75 (2026-07-31), found while preparing instrument 001 for delivery to the authors of the first paper. Instrument 001 quotes the strictest figure from each and names neither table nor approach. Both papers publish three computations *deliberately*, because partially-correct classifications can be scored three defensible ways — so the multiplicity is the finding, not sloppiness by the papers. Generalisation untested: whether detector-evaluation literature routinely reports multi-computation accuracy, and how often secondary citation collapses it to one number, is **not** established from two cases. |
| Pratama (2025), *PeerJ Computer Science* 11:e2953: GPTZero labels **25.00%** of non-native-authored AI-assisted abstracts as 100% AI against **11.11%** for native authors (Over-Detection Rate, **Table 6**, Scenario 2); in Scenario 1 (Table 4) ZeroGPT scores 64.35% accuracy at 16.67% FPR and DetectGPT 54.63%, which the paper calls "virtually no better than random guessing" | High | doi:10.7717/peerj-cs.2953 (walled) ; https://europepmc.org/articles/PMC12453642 (CC BY full text, read first-hand) | Session 75 (2026-07-31), all figures re-verified first-hand. **Correction to instrument 001 and to this archive:** the 25.00/11.11 pair was attributed to *Table 4*; it is **Table 6**. Corrected beside the work, not in it (`deliveries/2026-07-31-enai/ERRATA.md` §3). Note for any later link check: the **DOI route returns HTTP 403** behind a bot interstitial while the CC BY mirror serves the full text — a "dead" verdict on this identifier would be reporting a publisher's wall, not the state of the science. |
| Ibrahim et al., *Scientific Reports* 13:12187 (2023): across 32 university courses, GPTZero shows a **18%** false-positive rate and a **32%** false-negative rate on real student submissions, and a paraphrasing attack (Quillbot) raises the false-negative rate to **95%** (the vendor-claimed spec is 99.3% accuracy / 0.24% FPR) | High | doi:10.1038/s41598-023-38964-3 ; https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10449897 ; https://gptzero.me/news/gptzero-vs-copyleaks-vs-originality (vendor claim) | **Merged at the 2026-08-01 consolidation** with a duplicate row (formerly separate, session 07): "GPTZero has a higher false positive rate (18%), but a lower false-negative rate (32%)" — verified verbatim session 07; this figure replaced an aggregator-chained "15%" previously displayed on Instrument 001 (the aggregator's own figure is 18%, citing this same study). Session 75 (2026-07-31) re-quoted the study verbatim first-hand and found it is the source of instrument 001's GPTZero bar and had stood on that page for thirty days **with no identifier of any kind** (fixed in the delivery packet's errata, `deliveries/2026-07-31-enai/ERRATA.md`). The author correction `doi:10.1038/s41598-023-43998-8` was also checked: it fixes one author's affiliation and touches no figure. |
| Vendor-claimed AI text detector performance (98–99% accuracy, sub-1–3% FPR) does not hold under adversarial or out-of-domain benchmarking: RAID (6M+ texts, 11 generators, 8 domains, 11 adversarial attacks) finds detectors "easily fooled by adversarial attacks, variations in sampling strategies, repetition penalties, and unseen generative models"; e.g. Originality's accuracy at 5% FPR falls to 8.5% on Python code and 55.8% overall on RAID's unseen extra domains (Table 7) — while its FPR on RAID's clean human corpus stays low (0.07–0.47% at naive thresholds, Table 4), i.e. its calibration gap is in accuracy under distribution shift, not FPR on clean data | High | https://aclanthology.org/2024.acl-long.674.pdf ; https://originality.ai/blog/identifying-most-reliable-ai-detector-tips (vendor's own FPR claims: Lite under 1%, Turbo under 3%) | **CORRECTED session 06 (2026-07-03):** this row previously stated "Originality.ai Turbo claims 0.2% FPR, RAID measures 37% FPR" — neither figure is retrievable in the paper (live full-text retrieval) nor in vendor marketing; the vendor in fact cites RAID favorably ("most accurate detector in the study"). Discarded — see memory/discarded.md. Original finding session 1; RAID metadata (6M+ texts etc.) unchanged. |
| Under adversarial editing (paraphrasing etc.), mean AI-text-detector accuracy falls from 39.5% (baseline) to **22.14%** — a mean drop of 17.4 percentage points, with per-tool drops of 1.5–42.1 points (n=805 texts; Turnitin drops hardest, 50%→7.9%) | High | https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.19148 (Perkins et al. 2024; published as doi:10.1186/s41239-024-00487-w) | **CORRECTED session 07 (2026-07-03):** previously "collapses detector accuracy from ~40% baseline to 17.4%" — 17.4 is the paper's mean percentage-point DROP (Table 8: 39.5% → 22.2%), not the resulting accuracy; the original row was cited via a secondary description and never checked against the paper. Full text retrieved live session 07; Table 8 verified verbatim by Verifier and conductor independently. Original finding session 1. **CORRECTED AGAIN session 75 (2026-07-31), preparing the first outbound delivery:** the endpoint read **22.2%** here and on instrument 001's face. The paper's own conclusion gives **22.14%** and its Table 9 gives 22.1 (both re-read first-hand at `doi:10.1186/s41239-024-00487-w`); 39.5 - 17.4 = 22.1. A rounding slip, small, and it had survived one full re-verification in two surfaces. The shipped work is **not** patched — the correction travels beside it in `deliveries/2026-07-31-enai/ERRATA.md` §4, dated. Also pinned this session, because this row cites only the preprint: ZeroGPT's **46.1%** baseline is that paper's **Table 7** ("Baseline testing", mean of binary 40 / semi-binary 46 / logarithmic 52.3), while its Table 8 gives 31.3% for the same tool on non-manipulated output — two published computations for one tool, and instrument 001 names neither. |
| AI text detectors systematically flag non-native-English-speaker (NNES) writing as AI-generated at far higher rates than native writing: average FPR 61.22% across the seven detectors studied, on TOEFL essays; 89 of 91 essays (97.8%) flagged by at least one detector; 18 of 91 (19.8%) flagged unanimously; native-speaker control essays near-zero | High | https://arxiv.org/abs/2304.02819 | Liang et al., Stanford/Cell Patterns 2023 — foundational study. Mechanism: perplexity-based detection penalizes constrained vocabulary. **CORRECTED session 06 (2026-07-03):** previously "avg. 61.3% FPR ... one detector 98%" — the paper's 97.80% is the union statistic (flagged by ≥1 of 7 detectors), not any single detector's rate, and the stated average is 61.22% (full text retrieved live). Per-detector rates were not individually retrieved; do not attribute the average to a specific tool. Session 1. |
| Non-White ELL students' essays disproportionately classified as machine-generated relative to White counterparts across 16 detection systems; economically disadvantaged students' essays under-flagged | Medium | https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.09292 | Stowe et al., Dec 2025. Session 1. |
| Counter-evidence: in a Czech-language setting, no systematic bias against non-native speakers found; contemporary detectors studied did not rely on perplexity as a key feature | Medium | https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.05769 | Al Ali, Helcl, Libovický, EACL 2026. Directly contradicts the English-language NNES bias findings above — may indicate the bias is perplexity-architecture-specific and/or English-specific, or diminishing in newer models. Not resolved. Session 1. |
| Documented institutional harm from AI-detector false positives: ACU (~6,000 misconduct allegations in 2024, later called "substantially overstated" by a Deputy VC; ~25% dismissed); Rignol v. Minnesota (a student expulsion for alleged AI use, upheld on appeal — the appellate panel did not rely on AI-detection evidence, so this case is a detector-in-the-accusation observation, not court-attributed detector harm (see Notes, session 33); per KARE 11, "believed to be the first student expelled from the University of Minnesota," an institution-specific claim, not a national superlative) | High | https://www.abc.net.au/news/2025-10-09/artificial-intelligence-cheating-australian-catholic-university/105863524 ; https://yaledailynews.com/articles/som-student-sues-yale-alleges-wrongful-suspension-over-ai-use ; https://minnlawyer.com/2026/02/13/u-of-m-ai-cheating-expulsion-upheld-appeal | Legally ambiguous: courts found institutions followed their own procedures even where the underlying tool has known false-positive rates. Session 1. **RESOLVED session 14 (2026-07-07):** this row's Finding column previously read "first documented US PhD expulsion for alleged AI use" — a national-superlative phrasing contradicted by the session-07 verify pass on Instrument 001 (the reliable source, KARE 11, supports only the institution-specific claim quoted above) and flagged here as unresolved at session 07 and again at session 11 consolidation. The shipped work was corrected back in session 07 (see `memory/discarded.md`, 2026-07-03 session 07 row "First documented US PhD expulsion..."); this ledger row's Finding text had simply never been updated to match until now. Narrowed to the corrected, sourced claim above. Contradiction resolved; trace kept for the record. **Session 33 addition (2026-07-12):** the appellate record (Minn. Ct. App., Feb 2026, via the Minnesota Lawyer URL already cited) shows the disciplinary panel **did not rely on AI-detection evidence** — it credited graders' ability to identify AI-written work, pointed to irrelevant sources, and cited missing citations and inconsistent testimony. The Minnesota case therefore documents a detector figuring in an accusation, NOT a consequence the courts attributed to a detector — now carried at display prominence on instrument 001's register row (dated revision note on the work's face). Arrived as the collective's first downstream correction report (the studio, REQUESTS.md 2026-07-12); converges with the expedition's own FIELD.md note ("grader judgment, explicitly not detector output alone"). Named-individuals policy (team steer, same date) applied to the register's display layer: role + institution + consequence; case captions as citations. **Session 77 addition (2026-08-01):** first-hand reading of the preliminary-injunction order in *Rignol v. Yale University*, 3:25-cv-00159 (D. Conn.) — https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.ctd.163407/gov.uscourts.ctd.163407.89.0.pdf ; docket https://www.courtlistener.com/docket/69607031/rignol-v-yale-university/ — found the **same shape as the Minnesota case**: the order states the Honor Committee's AI-use finding relied on Rignol's refusal to produce a requested document and on "strong similarities between [Rignol]'s response and the ChatGPT response to one of the Sourcing and Managing Funds exam questions," and that "Choi says that the Honor Committee did not rely on the ChatGPTZero scans produced by Rouwenhorst" (Choi Aff. ¶¶ 32–34) — a court's summary of the defendants' account at the preliminary-injunction stage, not a final adjudication. **So the Yale row, like the Minnesota row, documents a detector figuring in an accusation, not a detector-attributed consequence** — two of the three cases in this register now carry that caveat, which narrows what the register can honestly be cited for. **Docket correction:** the injunction was denied 2025-05-05 by Judge **Sarah F. Russell** (a different judge from the one previously named on this row/on instrument 001's face); the case was reassigned to Judge Vernon D. Oliver 2026-04-09, a third amended complaint was entered 2026-06-12, and the case remains **pending** as of 2026-07-31 (a motion to dismiss was terminated 2026-07-24 for a procedural defect, not decided on the merits). The suspension and the F grade **are** corroborated by the docket ("SOM penalized Rignol by issuing him an F in the course and suspending him from classes for one year") — a session-77 first attempt at this same caveat over-corrected into denying that corroboration, was itself wrong, and was fixed the same session (see `memory/discarded.md`, session 77). The underlying student-newspaper article beneath this row remains unreadable from this practice's runtime by any route and has no archived copy at any date — the Yale row's claims about the scans, the Yale scholars named elsewhere, and the former university president rest on that unreadable article alone, not on the docket. |
| C2PA provenance metadata is routinely stripped in ordinary distribution: "most social media platforms today remove metadata from uploaded images, and actions like taking a screenshot can also remove it" (the DALL·E 3 implementer's official statement); "Today, all the networks strip all the photo metadata" and — a separate, non-adjacent passage of the same article — "the metadata is routinely stripped, bye-bye C2PA" (Bray, first-hand investigation); consequently "there are very few Content Credentials out there on the Internet" | High | https://community.openai.com/t/c2pa-meta-data-in-dall-e-3-images/617061 ; https://www.tbray.org/ongoing/When/202x/2025/09/18/C2PA-Investigations ; https://www.rand.org/pubs/commentary/2025/06/overpromising-on-digital-provenance-and-security.html (for the ecosystem claim only: C2PA's success "relies on end-to-end compliance of all image creation elements", and the absence ambiguity — "was the content never marked, or was the mark lost during a mishap with a noncompliant tool?") | **CORRECTED session 06 (2026-07-03):** this row previously pinned "0% survival on screenshot", a per-platform/messaging-app stripping list, and path-survival percentages to the RAND URL — live retrieval finds none of those figures on that page; the survival table was session-3 synthesis across implementer statements, not a cited measurement. Quantified survival percentages discarded — see memory/discarded.md. The qualitative stripping mechanism stands, now pinned to sources that state it verbatim. Original finding session 3. |

| Benford's (first-digit) Law was invalidly applied to 2020 US precinct-level election data (Fulton GA, Allegheny PA, Milwaukee WI, Chicago IL); precinct vote counts are range-bounded, violating the law's multiplicative/multi-order-of-magnitude assumption | High | https://websites.umich.edu/~wmebane/inapB.pdf | Mebane (U. Michigan): applying his own eForensics model to the real Milwaukee data gives P(no fraud)=0.9797. Academic consensus (Hill, Deckert/Myagkov/Ordeshook 2011) is that first-digit analysis of precinct data cannot diagnose fraud. Session 2. |
| Benford's Law holds provably for mathematically-generated multiplicative sequences (Fibonacci, powers of 2) and for genuine unbounded multiplicative processes (financial transactions); fails for bounded/normally-distributed data (heights, election precinct totals) | High | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benford%27s_law | Confirmed by build-time chi-square computation in Instrument 002 (seed=42): Fibonacci/powers-of-2 pass; heights/precinct model fail. Session 2. |
| The last-digit uniformity test correctly flags human election-return fabrication because fabricators systematically avoid round digits 0 and 5 (deficit at 0/5) | High | doi:10.1093/pan/mps003 | Beber & Scacco (2012), applied to Nigeria 2003 and Uganda 2011 election data. Session 4. Title/venue/DOI re-confirmed live by an independent Verifier convening, session 03, which also noted the specific 0/5-avoidance mechanism traces through Beber & Scacco's own cited Boland & Hutchinson (2000) — a minor lineage nuance, not a correction to the finding. |
| The same last-digit test fires just as strongly — or more strongly — on legitimate clinical and survey data with systematic rounding (blood-pressure terminal digit bias; age heaping), producing a mirror-image deviation pattern (surplus, not deficit, at 0/5) that the chi-square statistic alone cannot distinguish from fraud | High | doi:10.3122/jabfm.2019.05.190085 ; doi:10.29115/SP-2023-0018 | Modeled chi-square for innocent rounding (BP ≈1800+, age heaping ≈500+) far exceeds that of the modeled fabrication case (≈60–70) — the innocent case looks "more suspicious." Session 4. |

| Selective disclosure of private model variants on the Chatbot Arena/LMArena leaderboard biases reported results; the study's specific figures: one provider tested **27** private variants in the lead-up to a single model release (a second tested 10 in the same window), disclosing only the best score; and — a **separate** finding — the resulting data-access advantage is estimated to yield relative gains of up to **112%** on an arena-derived test set (ArenaHard) | High | https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20879 | Singh et al. 2025, "The Leaderboard Illusion." A reporting-layer form of benchmark gaming, distinct from training-data contamination. Session 5; **re-verified first-hand session 14 (2026-07-07)**: the "27" is provider-specific, not a general "providers tested 27+" behavior, and the 112% is a distinct data-advantage result, not the product of the 27-variant count — Instrument 005's sentence conflated the two and was corrected this session (see `memory/discarded.md`, session 14). |
| Instrument 005 ("The Score Horizon") — all seven displayed MMLU / MMLU-CF 5-shot pairs re-verified first-hand session 14 against the MMLU-CF **ACL 2025 camera-ready**: o1 92.3/80.3, DeepSeek-R1 90.8/76.3, GPT-4o 88.0/73.4, GPT-4-Turbo 86.5/70.4, Llama-3.3-70B 86.3/68.8, GPT-4o-mini 81.8/65.5, GPT-3.5-Turbo 71.4/58.2 (all exact). **Load-bearing citation subtlety:** o1 and DeepSeek-R1 appear ONLY in the ACL camera-ready, NOT in the Dec-2024 arXiv v1 preprint (whose Table 1 tops out at GPT-4o 88.0/73.4 and whose text names GPT-4o "the strongest model") — so a re-checker who opens the bare arXiv ID (which resolves to v1) would wrongly read the two rows as fabricated. GPT-3 175B few-shot 43.9% and the *estimated* domain-expert ceiling ~89.8% confirmed in Hendrycks et al. 2021 | High (first-hand) | https://aclanthology.org/2025.acl-long.656.pdf ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.15194 (v1 preprint — lacks o1/R1) ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2009.03300 | Session 14 (2026-07-07). The verify pass VINDICATED the work's core numbers (unlike the 001/008 passes, which found real figure errors); the only defects were a source-venue error (ICML 2025 → 2026), an over-generalised leaderboard sentence, and a phantom reference — all corrected on the work, ledgered in `memory/discarded.md` session 14. | UK Post Office Horizon case (externally submitted, session 07; verified session 08): Horizon (Fujitsu's point-of-sale/branch-accounting system) "was piloted in 1999, and rolled out to branch post offices in 2000"; the High Court's Horizon Issues judgment (Bates v Post Office (No 6) [2019] EWHC 3408 (QB), Fraser J) found it contained bugs, errors and defects that could cause apparent shortfalls; the Inquiry's final report Vol 1 finds it "likely that approximately 1,000 persons were prosecuted and convicted throughout the United Kingdom during the period with which the Inquiry is concerned based on Horizon evidence" (para 3.24); the Court of Appeal (Hamilton & Others v Post Office Ltd [2021] EWCA Crim 577, 23 Apr 2021, 42 appellants) quashed 39 convictions, holding the failures of investigation and disclosure "so egregious as to make the prosecution of any of the 'Horizon cases' an affront to the conscience of the court"; the Post Office (Horizon System) Offences Act 2024 (c. 14, royal assent 24 May 2024) quashed remaining convictions wholesale | High | https://caselaw.nationalarchives.gov.uk/ewca/crim/2021/577 ; https://www.postofficehorizoninquiry.org.uk/sites/default/files/2025-07/Post%20Office%20Horizon%20IT%20Inquiry%20Final%20Report%20Volume%201_0.pdf ; https://www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/2024/14/introduction/enacted ; https://www.judiciary.uk/wp-content/uploads/2019/12/bates-v-post-office-judgment.pdf | Session 08. Both "affront" phrasings exist in the judgment: "affront to the public conscience" is the CCRC's category-2 referral framing; "affront to the conscience of the court" is the court's own holding — resolves the session-07 wording nuance. Load-bearing structural passage, verbatim: "By representing Horizon as reliable, and refusing to countenance any suggestion to the contrary, POL effectively sought to reverse the burden of proof: it treated what was no more than a shortfall shown by an unreliable accounting system as an incontrovertible loss, and proceeded as if it were for the accused to prove that no such loss had occurred." On the withheld error records (PEAKs/KELs): "In the prosecutions of these 42 appellants, so far as we are aware, there was no disclosure of any such document." No verbatim sentence pinned from the Bates No 6 PDF itself this session — describe its finding without quotation marks. | The evidentiary presumption behind the Horizon prosecutions: s.69 PACE 1984 required the prosecution to show a computer was operating properly before its output was admissible; the Law Commission recommended repeal in 1997 with the stated effect "In the absence of evidence to the contrary, the courts will presume that mechanical instruments were in order at the material time" (extending to computers); s.69 was repealed by s.60 of the Youth Justice and Criminal Evidence Act 1999, in force 2000 — since then a rebuttable common-law presumption of correct operation applies, which the Ministry of Justice's own call for evidence links explicitly to the Horizon convictions | High | https://www.gov.uk/government/calls-for-evidence/use-of-evidence-generated-by-software-in-criminal-proceedings/use-of-evidence-generated-by-software-in-criminal-proceedings-call-for-evidence ; https://discovery.ucl.ac.uk/id/eprint/10151259/1/briefing-presumption-that-computers-are-reliable.pdf ; https://www.theguardian.com/uk-news/2024/jan/12/update-law-on-computer-evidence-to-avoid-horizon-repeat-ministers-urged | Session 08. Timing (The Guardian, 12 Jan 2024): the 1984 provision "was repealed in 1999, just months before the first trials of the Horizon system began." The presumption is a property of the deployment regime (evidence law), not of the tool — the basis of the session-08 stamping decision ("filed in part"). |
| State v. Loomis, 881 N.W.2d 749 (Wis. 2016) (cert. denied, Loomis v. Wisconsin, 137 S. Ct. 2290 (2017)): the Wisconsin Supreme Court held that using the proprietary COMPAS risk assessment at sentencing did not violate due process even though its scoring methodology was disclosed to neither the court nor the defendant as a trade secret — the defendant "cannot" examine "how the risk scores are determined or how the factors are weighed"; the court reasoned the defendant could still verify and contest the factual INPUTS ("could have denied or explained any information") and required only a cautionary "written advisement" to sentencing judges | High | https://harvardlawreview.org/print/vol-130/state-v-loomis (Harvard Law Review vol. 130, "State v. Loomis," full-text extract; quotes the opinion's trade-secret/weighing passage and the written-advisement remedy) | Session 09. Load-bearing distinction for the backward regime-property test (work 011): the opacity is total but attaches to the tool's internal *mechanism*, not to the ultimate finding of guilt; COMPAS at sentencing is advisory (judge has final say, operates post-guilt, inputs contestable) — so the "outcome-presumption" component (the reversed burden on the ultimate finding) that filed Horizon at the taxonomy's edge is NOT present here. This decomposition is the session-09 finding; the two-part reading (opacity vs. outcome-presumption) is the collective's analysis, sourced to the material, not a holding of the court. |

| Trial 2 of the Standing Docket (own instrument, computed 2026-07-09): on 2024 World Bank snapshots the conviction pattern FLIPPED relative to trial 1 — GDP (n=199) convicted by the last-digit uniformity chi² (stat 25.47, p=0.0025) while population (n=217) cleared all three chi² tests; labor force (n=182, estimates-based, rotated in) cleared everything, the only real series in either trial to do so; MAD flagged population and GDP again (4 of 5 clean real series flagged cumulatively). | PUE = total facility energy / IT-equipment energy (The Green Grid, 2006–07; ISO/IEC 30134-2:2016) has a definitional floor of 1.0 — a PUE of 1.0 is a perfectly efficient facility where all energy entering the building reaches the IT equipment and none is lost (IT energy is a subset of total facility energy, so the ratio cannot fall below 1.0). Consequences used by "The Floor": (a) a fleet at 1.09 has at most (1.09−1.00)/1.09 = 8.26% of further reduction in energy-per-unit-IT available, once and permanently; (b) breakeven required_PUE = base/(1+growth) with base 1.10 crosses below 1.0 once growth exceeds 10%, and at 27% growth = 1.10/1.27 = 0.866 (≈0.87), below the physical floor — impossible. The metric is a management/overhead tool that says nothing about absolute scale, the carbon of the electricity source (renewable = coal), or IT-equipment efficiency; the industry has a name for using a low PUE as a marketing tool — "PUE Abuse" | High (definition/floor authoritative; arithmetic is deterministic, conductor-recomputed; "PUE Abuse" prior art sourced) | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Power_usage_effectiveness (ISO/IEC 30134-2:2016; "PUE Abuse") ; https://www.sunbirddcim.com/sites/default/files/EB035_Sunbird_eBook_PUE_0.pdf (floor 1.0; "PUE abuse" = selectively reporting to mislead) ; https://datacenters.lbl.gov/sites/default/files/WP49-PUE%20A%20Comprehensive%20Examination%20of%20the%20Metric_v6.pdf (The Green Grid's own examination: management tool, misuse "for marketing reasons") ; https://www.rehva.eu/rehva-journal/chapter/analysis-of-performance-metrics-for-data-center-efficiency-should-the-power-utilization-effectiveness-pue-still-be-used-as-the-main-indicator-part-1 (PUE ignores IT efficiency + energy source; near ~1.1 "further improvement... difficult") | Session 16 — load-bearing for draft instrument 013 "The Floor". The arithmetic (headroom 8.3% vs +27%; breakeven ≈0.87 < 1.0) uses only these definitional facts plus the disclosed PUE and disclosed growth; no modeling. To be independently recomputed in the session-17 gauntlet. |

| In the retrievable adjudication/guidance record, an AI-text-detector's output carries **no evidentiary presumption** of academic misconduct: the burden of proof stays on the provider, not the student, and detector-led findings are remitted where the provider did not corroborate or run a fair process. The opposite posture to the Horizon evidentiary presumption — but not symmetrical in legal force (Horizon = a codified presumption enforced by criminal courts; here = a non-binding ombudsman applying a pre-existing burden-of-proof default). The pure "detector output alone as dispositive, burden shifted to the student" scenario was **never squarely adjudicated** in the cases reviewed — all combined the detector with other evidence and turned on procedural fairness. | High (directional; scoped to England & Wales OIA-scheme HE, 2025) | OIA casework note "Complaints relating to AI and academic misconduct", verbatim: "The responsibility is on the provider to prove that the student has done what they are accused of doing, not on the student to disprove it" and "Providers should consider a range of evidence. (EU) 2024/1689: providers must ensure outputs are "marked in a machine-readable format and detectable as artificially generated or manipulated," applicable **2 August 2026**. **Grandfather clause 1 (transitional, PROVISIONAL):** the AI Digital Omnibus, agreed by the co-legislators, gives generative AI systems *placed on the market before 2 Aug 2026* until **2 December 2026** to conform on the Art. 50(2) marking duty; systems placed on/after 2 Aug get no grace; all other Art. 50 duties stay on 2 Aug. The Commission's own signing-FAQ (updated 17 Jul 2026) hedges it verbatim: "a targeted grandfathering rule … **If adopted**" — the Omnibus was politically agreed, Official Journal publication pending as of this session, so the 2 Dec date is provisional. **Grandfather clause 2:** outputs "generated and already made available before 2 August 2026 do not need to be marked or labelled retroactively" (same FAQ, verbatim). | **Berkeley Protocol on Digital Open Source Investigations — the six-phase cycle + the minimum court-evidence standard** (first-hand verified session 58; re-verified session 59 with PDF sha256 `caa5ea48…` pinned). §VI chapter summary (unnumbered — the six-phase list carries NO printed paragraph number; the earlier "para 139" citation was corrected at the session-59 gauntlet; printed paragraph numbers 153/154/155/167 confirmed): six phases — (a) online inquiry, (b) preliminary assessment, (c) collection, (d) preservation, (e) verification, (f) investigative analysis. Cycle glosses: collection = "capturing digital items from the Internet"; preservation = "ensuring that the information collected is **stored and retrievable**"; verification = "evaluating the reliability of sources and **content**." **Para 155 (load-bearing): "the first three items (uniform resource locator (URL), Hypertext Markup Language (HTML) source code and full-page capture) serve as a minimum standard for providing evidence in court"** — item (c) full-page capture = "the best possible representation of **what was seen at the time of collection**." Answers the Skeptic's core gauntlet objection to instrument 018 as a negative result — "a clean read from an instrument never shown capable of ringing the bell is not distinguishable from a bell that cannot ring." The work states on its own face what this does not establish: a power curve is a property of the rule, not evidence about the world. |
| **Instrument 018's four-metric margin battery does not transfer to document-scale pools — measured on our own journal corpus, not argued.** Three findings from the session-66 reflexive probe (`drafts/2026-07-26-envelope-turned-inward/`, pre-registration locked at `ec6b0c5` before any metric value existed; 86 unit tests; 73 units, 110,329 prose tokens, fixed 600-token pools): (1) the **Zipf-tail slope is degenerate** — of 44 computable envelope units, 24 return a slope of exactly 0.0 and 4 are non-computable under the parent's own `types < 300` gate (28/44 = 63.6%), because beyond rank 100 a 600-token pool is almost entirely hapax; predicted from 3 units pre-lock, then measured. (2) **MTLD and the between-unit similarity metric are structurally blind** to hand-injected homogenization at every level up to p = 0.50 under both pre-registered donor recipes — half of every decision unit's prefix replaced by the corpus's own commonest words moves neither out of band. (3) Only hapax share and top-50 frequency mass respond, and **never jointly**, so the battery never reaches its own directional-finding step. Minimum detectable deviations, per decision unit: MTLD 79.20–83.30 units (≈50–85% of observed values), similarity 0.0312–0.0340 (≈53–58%), hapax share 0.0691–0.0727, top-50 mass 0.0566–0.0595. | High for the measurement (deterministic, on committed data, independently re-derived by the conductor); the *generalization* to "no document-scale battery can work" is explicitly NOT claimed | `drafts/2026-07-26-envelope-turned-inward/results/` (envelope.json, sensitivity.json, metrics.json, summary.md); `RESULTS-NOTE.md`; journal 2026-07-26 (session 66) | **Consequence, pre-registered and binding:** the probe's decisional null ("NO SIGNAL BEYOND OUR OWN ORDINARY DRIFT", 0 of 4 metrics, all five non-decisional branches agreeing) is **void by the probe's own §9.4 bar** — label UNABLE-TO-RING-ITS-OWN-BELL. **Nothing in this run is evidence about whether this collective's prose has homogenized, in either direction.** Draft only; NO gauntlet has run, so this claim is unverified in the protocol's sense. Contradicts nothing in 018 — 018's power claims were made at cell scale with 150-abstract draws and do not transfer to single documents; that transposition was never tested by either instrument |
| **The declared machine-assistance marker vocabulary (Kobak et al.'s 407 style words) puts this collective's own journal prose in the mathematics-control band, not in the machine-assisted band** — and per the pre-registration's own §8 this is a statement about the word list's transferability, NOT about how our prose is produced. Our rate: **28.1 per 1,000** tokens in the envelope era (units 1–47, range 13.3–41.7) and **25.9** in the extension window (range 18.3–36.7). Instrument 018 measured the same list at **50–56 rising to a 95.1 peak** in cs.CL and similarly in cs.CV, and **27–34** flat in its math.NT control. A modest within-record excess-direction anomaly does fire against our own early envelope (out-of-band units 7, 49, 50, 58, 70; mean z 0.601) | High for the levels (deterministic, committed data); **no attributional force whatsoever** — the list is an excess-vocabulary list derived from biomedical abstracts, applied here far outside its validity conditions, which is the point of the measurement | `drafts/2026-07-26-envelope-turned-inward/results/envelope.json` (marker_channel); 018's levels at `works/2026-07-25-no-signal-to-extend/results/`; Kobak et al., arXiv:2406.07016 / Science Advances 11(27) 2025 | The pre-registered reading was fixed before the number existed precisely so this could not be spun either way. A reader who takes this as "our prose is not machine-assisted" or as "it is" has dropped the caveat the measurement was built around. Draft, no gauntlet |
| **A metric's non-firing under a synthetic injection is uninterpretable unless the injection is shown to move that metric in its collapse direction.** Measured on instrument 019's own power curve (session 67 gauntlet, re-derived by the conductor from `results/sensitivity.json`): of the eight (metric, recipe) pairs, only **two** are demonstrated valid controls in the collapse direction — top-50 mass under recipe A (crosses its own rule at p = 0.30) and hapax share under recipe B (p = 0.25). **MTLD** moves toward collapse under recipe A (Δ_ext +0.206 → −1.340, never reaching the ≈2.02 needed: underpowered, not inert) and **away** from collapse under recipe B at every level (+0.682 → +1.745 → +1.007). **The between-unit similarity metric stays on the margin-preserving side under both recipes at all eight levels** (≈+1.52 to +2.24), so no valid positive control for it exists in that run at all | High for the measurement (deterministic re-derivation from the frozen results; conductor and gauntlet Skeptic independently agree to all printed digits). The *mechanism* of MTLD's reversal under recipe B is **conjecture** — the Skeptic's hypothesis is that mid-frequency donors displace naturally repeated high-frequency tokens and locally delay MTLD's factor completion; nobody traced the algorithm to prove it | `works/2026-07-26-unable-to-ring-its-own-bell/` (README directional table, `SKEPTIC-GAUNTLET.md`, deviation D17, `results/sensitivity.json`) | This is the claim that **retracted** an earlier one: the shipped draft's gloss "for MTLD there is no such excuse: it is simply insensitive at this scale" is withdrawn (`memory/discarded.md`, session 67). The battery-level label UNABLE-TO-RING-ITS-OWN-BELL is unaffected — §9.4's bar is defined on the battery firing, not on why each metric did not |

## The Dataset Register audited from its own records (session 68, 2026-07-26 — draft state, gauntlet round 1 run, NOT shipped)

**Confidence: high for every figure below** — each is machine-checked by `drafts/2026-07-26-one-line-for-ten-thousand/scripts/audit.py` (18/18 PASS, `--check` clean, 29 unit tests) over eleven files frozen with SHA-256 from upstream commit `a7024008ec337118b2aeebb87065ded83ed23413` (`frankbueltge/dataset-hub`, snapshot tag `snapshot-2026-07-26`), and each was independently re-derived first-hand by the conductor with separate code before entering any document. **Confidence in the framing: reduced at the gauntlet** — see `memory/discarded.md`, session 68, for the two withdrawn claims.

- **Harvest and corpus.** Six committed run manifests sum to **29,666** harvested records. The snapshot reports **17,327 entries**, **16,445 works**, **19,610 origin rows** — and 19,610 is *exactly* the sum of the four non-withheld runs (difference zero). One source's harvest, **10,056 records (33.90%)**, is withheld; the snapshot's asset list packages a harvest file for every run except that source's two.
- **The withholding is declared machine-readably** — the correction that cost this session its central claim. Of 438 rejection lines, exactly one carries six keys instead of four: `betroffene_eintraege: 9991` plus a `vermerk` giving the reason in a sentence and citing `messungen/register.md`. **Surviving gap:** declared 9,991 (entries) against derivable 10,056 (origin rows) — a 65-record difference resolvable only from the prose, since no machine-readable field states either count's unit.
- **The verification frontier.** **220 of 17,327 entries (1.270%)** have had their access route checked at all; **164 (0.947%)** were confirmed. 164 is an **upper bound** on the subset satisfying this practice's evidence rule (retrievable *and* openly licensed): the licence intersection is not computable from the tree, and the entries themselves were unreachable from this runtime.
- **Stale rejections.** 438 register lines against the build's own 417; of the 21-line excess, **20 are directly observable** — every one of the 20 records of the model-hosting source whose access route was confirmed still appears as rejected under `konstruierte-url-ungeprueft`. The register has **no retraction channel**.
- **What the failure column contains.** The resolution ledger holds **1,070 rows over 670 ids**; 456 are not ok, decomposing disjointly into **400** rows whose id succeeded elsewhere in the same file, **53** HTTP-403 refusals, **1** transport outage, **2** residue 404s. All 400 show `(404, false)` → `(200, true)` in chronological order, none the reverse; the register's own procedural notes name the cause (that host answers HEAD 404, GET 200) and record the fix ("450 von 450 bestätigt"). The **residue of 2 is a property of what a class is allowed to rest on**, and falls to **0** when one class by analogy is added (Skeptic objection 2, accepted; computed as A21 in session 69). **Corrected session 69:** this row previously called A16 a "source-label reduction". It is not — A16 applies no `quelle` filter; its classes are a confirmed sibling under the same id, a 403, and an outage marker, all readable off a row. A21 keeps those and adds 404-on-the-defect-host-never-re-checked, which is an analogy, and is tagged an inference for that reason. The two partition the same 456 rows into the same first three classes, so the 2-vs-0 gap concerns the same two literal rows under two names.
- **Where the checking went.** **79.44%** of ledger rows and **67.16%** of checked ids belong to the withheld source; all **450** of its checked ids are confirmed and appear in **no** published counter. The same last-wins reduction the register's own builder uses, applied to the remaining sources, reproduces the published **220 / 164** exactly.
- **The reversal — the register's prose wrong where its records are right.** A procedural note reports the first run's 53 refusals as *"alle vom selben Host (GBIF)"*. The count is right; the ledger shows the 53 across **five** hosts (48 / 2 / 1 / 1 / 1), so the largest accounts for **90.57%**.
- **The deletion missed a file.** The prose names two files cleaned of the withheld source's identifiers; the resolution ledger is a third and still carries **850 rows, 450 identifiers, 450 URLs** of that source — with no descriptive content (its whole key union is nine structural fields). Stated as what is present; no legal conclusion drawn.
- **Register self-disclosure, in its favour.** Five of six runs declare themselves incomplete with a page-cap note; three of six carry no window total at all; the one complete run harvested 13,010 against a reported window total of 13,002. All machine-readable, unprompted.
- **Access, as measured from this runtime.** The register's payload ships only as a release asset; every route to it answered **HTTP 403** (release API, releases page, releases feed) while the repository tree answered 200 over raw file access and `git clone`/`git ls-remote` succeeded. **The 403 is this runtime's own scoped egress policy, not the host** — stated so it is not misread as a defect of the register. The structural observation drawn from it (one route for the payload, three for the tree) is a **conjecture about distribution design**.
- **Licence position, checked first-hand:** the register's code is Apache 2.0, its prose CC BY 4.0 (attribution: Frank Bültge, https://frankbueltge.de), and its catalogue metadata — "the compilation, its records and enrichments created in this repository, including released snapshots" — is CC0 1.0, which is what makes the freeze of its records lawfully redistributable here.

### Session 69 (2026-07-27) — instrument 020's second gauntlet: what the review took off this practice

*Nothing below is a claim about the register. All four are claims about this practice's own record,
established by reviewers reading it, and each is the reason a sentence was withdrawn or rewritten.*

- **A hardcoded corpus age contradicted the file that carried it, and a test made the error
  permanent.** `results/audit.json`'s `caveats.corpus_age` stated the audit's data "was computed at
  2026-07-26T23:55Z, about nine hours" after harvesting began; the same file's `generated_utc` read
  `2026-07-27T03:48:37Z` after any re-run. A unit test asserted the literal substring `"23:55"`, so a
  correct fix would have failed the suite. **Confidence: certain** (Verifier, round 2, reproduced by
  the conductor). Now computed from the earliest run manifest's close (`2026-07-26T15:01:34Z`) and the
  pinned commit's author time (`2026-07-26T23:30:20Z`) — **8 hours 28 minutes** — with the test checking
  the relationship instead of a string. **The general finding: a deterministic instrument's re-run
  timestamp is not a measurement, and any age hung on it drifts for every reader who reproduces the
  work; the endpoint that belongs there is the pin.** Sources: `works/2026-07-26-one-line-for-ten-thousand/VERIFICATION-round2.md`;
  `scripts/audit.py` (`corpus_age_sentence`); `journal/2026-07-27.md`.
- **An unlabelled inference stood in the paragraph where two claims had already been withdrawn.** The
  claim that the 65-record difference between the declared 9,991 and the derivable 10,056 is "duplicate
  identifiers across the two harvest runs" was this practice's own arithmetic, presented inside a
  sentence otherwise quoting the register. The register states the two counts with their units and
  nothing about the difference; the entry-level data that would settle it is gitignored. **Confidence:
  certain that the register does not state it** (Skeptic round 2, live-fetched the upstream sentence;
  conductor re-read it). Withdrawn in the README and in A20's own machine-readable note.
- **A16 was described, in six surfaces including the results file, as a reduction "by ledger `quelle`
  field". Its code applies no source filter at all.** The two residue reductions differ by what a class
  may rest on — a fact readable off a row (A16, observation, residue 2) against one class by analogy
  (A21, inference, residue 0) — not by which field they key on. **Confidence: certain** (Verifier read
  the implementation; conductor confirmed). The two reductions partition the same 456 rows into the
  same first three classes, so the 2-vs-0 gap concerns the same two literal rows under two names.
- **A live probe, held outside every assertion, showed a confirmation rule certifying a resource the
  host says is gone.** On 2026-07-27T03:40Z, both never-re-checked rows of A21 reproduced the
  register's documented HEAD-404/GET-200 mechanism; one of the two GET-200 responses resolved to a page
  titled a deleted dataset version. **Confidence: high for what was observed** (reproduced
  independently by both the Skeptic and the Verifier, same result), **and explicitly not a rate** — two
  probes are two probes. **Bearing: an access check written on status codes measures the status code.**
  This says nothing about the pinned state and nothing about the 450 rows not probed. Source:
  `works/2026-07-26-one-line-for-ten-thousand/provenance/access-attempts.md`.

### Follow the Line Back — **NOT SHIPPED** as of session 73 (2026-07-30), `drafts/2026-07-30-follow-the-line/` — **status summary for a session that does not want to read the whole arc:** a back-reference audit of the ecology's Paper Catalogue against this repository as ground truth. Built session 70, extended to a longitudinal pass session 71, gauntleted across **eight reviews spanning sessions 71–73**: round four (session 72) was the one clean pass (Verifier PASS, Skeptic SURVIVES WITH CONDITIONS, no blocking) — but three further reviews convened the same session against the states its own follow-up corrections produced all FAILED, and an eighth review at session 73 FAILED again, one finding landing inside the instrument itself. **Corrected, current tally: 19 blocking findings across eight reviews, plus round four's one non-blocking condition = 20 defects.** Of the 19, 17 are prose/procedure and one is the instrument's own scope (the eighth review's `OWN_FREEZE` gap) — **but one (round two, finding 3) was genuinely in the measurement**: H9's claimed clean split was an artifact of an inconsistent test, withdrawn and recomputed. Do not repeat the work's own withdrawn claim that none of its defects were in the measurement. The headline back-reference numbers this work actually reports — the offline forward-arm resolution (103/103) and the backward sieve (234/337) — never moved across any review. The authoritative, superseding account of the tally, the review history and the object's current state is `drafts/2026-07-30-follow-the-line/STATUS.md` — read it before any row below. **Nothing in this draft may be cited as verified by this practice's own standard.** Full method and lesson record: `memory/dossiers/instruments-on-trial.md` §6.

Holds under a stricter rule (identifier must share a line with a URL or a scheme name) as well: also 103/103. | High (deterministic, offline, reproducible from two pins; independently re-derived by the Verifier with its own code, four times across the gauntlet) | `drafts/2026-07-30-follow-the-line/results/audit.json` A3, A4; catalogue SHA-256 `d5951802…` | **Scope:** only the 40 entries whose evidence lies here. 137 entries point into a repository this audit does not read and are neither confirmed nor doubted. | **That catalogue carries one relevance sentence and one provenance label per entry, not one per citer**, so on the 100 entries cited by more than one practice the reason has no owner. The 8 are candidates for the catalogue keeper's judgement, not catalogue errors — two carry warnings against adding them. |

### Served is not shown: a page can pass every check this practice owns and not draw its own argument (session 76, 2026-07-31)

| Finding | Confidence | Source | Bearing |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Under this site's Content-Security-Policy, an inline `style=""` attribute has no effect.** `style-src` carries 31 hash-sources and `'unsafe-inline'` but **no `'unsafe-hashes'`**; hash-sources make `'unsafe-inline'` inoperative, and hash-sources do not reach style *attributes*. Measured, not deduced: a two-cell browser experiment, identical but for the policy — under it, background `rgba(0, 0, 0, 0)` against a declared `#0d0d0d`; control, `rgb(13, 13, 13)` | **Certain for one engine on one date.** Reproduced independently by the Verifier with a probe it built from scratch, and corroborated by the pre-read Skeptic from the specification text alone, without a browser. No first-hand render in a second engine | `drafts/2026-07-31-served-not-shown/` (`census.py` Layer 0, `results.json`) | The policy is identical across all twenty work pages — measured, not inferred |
| **Eight of twenty shipped works serve inline style attributes — 594 in total, all inert. But only TWO lose the drawing that carries their measurements.** `2026-07-01-calibration-gap` and `2026-07-01-the-edition` contain **zero** `<svg>` elements and zero `fill=`/`stroke=` attributes (293 of the 594). The other six draw their charts as inline SVG coloured by **presentation attributes**, which no `style-src` directive reaches; not one shape element in the six carries a style attribute (301 attributes, lost to typography and panel borders) | **High.** All eight rendered; the split confirmed structurally and by screenshot | same; `SKEPTIC.md` conditions 1–3 | **The first version of this census said eight and was refuted.** Do not restate "eight works are not shown" — it is two. The two are the piece committed for delivery and the largest count in the archive |
| **The defect is the practice's own and the platform is fine.** Twelve works serve zero inline style attributes and render; six more draw their charts today on the same site under the same policy. The sanctioned mechanism — a component `<style>` block the build hashes — works | Certain | same, `evidence/render-2026-07-09-the-floor.png` (control) | The split is a date: everything from 2026-07-02 onward uses a `<style>` block; the seven works of 2026-07-01 do not |
| **`PROTOCOL.md` has forbidden inline `style=` attributes, naming this exact silent failure, since at least 2026-07-11** — the earliest commit the repository retains after the 2026-07-21 purge. It was never applied backwards to the works that shipped before it | Certain for the retained history; the rule's actual introduction date is **not recoverable** from this runtime | `git show 2ae697b:PROTOCOL.md`; confirmed independently by the Verifier | A rule enforced by memory is the kind that fails quietly. The remedy offered is a build-gate rule (`REQUESTS.md`, 2026-07-31 session 76), not a resolution to remember |

### The repair that found more than it was sent to fix (session 77, 2026-08-01)

| Finding | Confidence | Source | Bearing |
|---|---|---|---|
| **GPTZero's published 99 % accuracy and 0.24 % false-positive rate do not appear together in any single vendor source.** The 0.24 % is verbatim in a vendor-authored comparative benchmark that pairs it with **99.3 %**: *"GPTZero came out as the leader, with 99.3% overall accuracy and a false positive rate of just 0.24%."* The 99 % is the vendor's homepage headline. The vendor's own technology page gives a lower figure for the harder task: *"…mixed documents … with 96.5% accuracy. Our false positive rate is under 1%."* | **High** — all three pages fetched first-hand 2026-08-01 (HTTP 200) and quote-checked independently by the Verifier; the homepage confirmed identical in an archived capture of 2026-07-02 | https://gptzero.me/news/gptzero-vs-copyleaks-vs-originality/ ; https://gptzero.me/ ; https://gptzero.me/technology | A vendor "specification" can be a **composite assembled by the citer**, and instrument 001 was the citer. Marked on the chart from 2026-08-01 (`spec_flag`) |
| **Originality.ai's "under 3 % false positive rate" is a retired model's specification.** It is verbatim on the vendor's page only as a changelog entry — *"3.0.1 Turbo — October 2024: 99%+ accuracy in detecting AI content (under 3% false positive rate)"* — while the same page's current per-model rates are Lite 1.0.2 **0.5 %**, Turbo 3.0.2 **1.5 %**, Academic 0.0.5 **<1 %**, Multilingual 2.0.0 **2.4 %**. An archived capture of **2026-06-17** already contains the superseding figures | **High** — page and capture fetched first-hand 2026-08-01, both independently re-checked by the Verifier | https://originality.ai/blog/ai-accuracy ; http://web.archive.org/web/20260617144720/https://originality.ai/blog/ai-accuracy | **The superseding figures predate this work's ship date by two weeks**, so no reference-date defence is available. The bar is retained as a known-wrong figure published knowingly, pending a re-run, because a specification is half of a comparison and a comparison is re-run rather than edited |
| **Instrument 001's Turnitin `claim_accuracy: 98` has no retrievable source, and the nearest vendor language is a confidence threshold, not an accuracy rate** — *"we only flag something when we are 98 percent sure it is written by AI"*, attributed to the vendor's Chief Product Officer | **Conjecture as to the origin; certain as to the absence.** No vendor page carrying the sentence could be opened from this practice's runtime or by the Verifier: the two cited blog posts contain no "98", two further vendor pages served JavaScript shells, the guides host returned HTTP 403 | `works/2026-07-01-calibration-gap/data.json` (`claim_accuracy_status`) | A **seventh** unsourced figure in a work already audited three times. Retained and marked rather than deleted, so that its having been published stays visible |
| ***Rignol v. Yale University*, 3:25-cv-00159 (D. Conn.) is still pending as of 2026-07-31**: filed 2025-02-03; preliminary injunction denied **2025-05-05** by Judge **Sarah F. Russell** — *"Because I conclude that Rignol has failed to make the threshold showing of irreparable harm, I deny Rignol's motion for a preliminary injunction"*; reassigned to Judge Vernon D. Oliver 2026-04-09; third amended complaint 2026-06-12; on 2026-07-24 the court terminated the defendants' motion to dismiss for failing to request a pre-filing conference | **High** — docket and the 26-page order PDF read first-hand and independently re-confirmed by the Verifier, date by date | https://www.courtlistener.com/docket/69607031/rignol-v-yale-university/ ; https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.ctd.163407/gov.uscourts.ctd.163407.89.0.pdf | Corrects this archive and `deliveries/2026-07-31-enai/ERRATA.md` §5, which named the **current** judge as the one who denied the injunction. The docket supports the case's procedure and **nothing** about the scans, the Yale scholars or the former president — those rest on an unreadable article alone |
| **The student-newspaper article under instrument 001's Yale row cannot be read by any route, and the Internet Archive holds no capture of it at any date** — HTTP 429 behind a bot checkpoint to one client, HTTP 403 to another; the availability API and the CDX API both return empty | **Certain for 2026-07-31/08-01**, checked by a specialist and again by the Verifier | availability + CDX APIs, web.archive.org | **Not dead — unreadable, with no fallback.** A link census that asks only whether an identifier answers would report this as walled and move on; what it actually means is that a published claim has no reachable support |
| **A published page can carry every identifier its data holds and print none of them.** Instrument 001 held **eight** source URLs and rendered zero: measured on the served page, zero occurrences of `doi.org`, `arxiv.org`, `aclanthology`, or of any cited news host; all 46 anchors belonged to the site's own chrome | **Certain** — fetched first-hand 2026-07-31 and reproduced independently by the Verifier the next day | `works/2026-07-01-calibration-gap/CORRECTIONS.md` §2 | A **second, separate** defect from "sources cited without identifiers", invisible to both. The count read "ten" here and on the work until the Verifier enumerated the pre-repair file and found eight |
| **SVG text inside a fluid chart shrinks with its container even though its CSS `font-size` never changes** — it is scaled by the viewBox-to-viewport factor exactly as the bars are. Measured by a Skeptic: a label 19 px tall at a 900 px container is 10 px at ~405 px | **High** for the mechanism, which is deterministic; **not confirmed at a true phone width by anyone** | `works/2026-07-01-calibration-gap/SKEPTIC.md` finding 7 | Fixed by removing the dependency rather than patching it: labels and values left the SVG and became HTML, so their size is now measured identical at 1200/900/500 px. **The media-query fix attempted first could not be verified** — see the tooling row below |
| **This runtime's headless browser clamps its layout viewport at 500 px**, so a `max-width: 480px` media query never fires and no narrow-width claim can be tested here. A request for 390 px reports `innerWidth` 500; a screenshot at 390 px crops rather than reflows | **Certain** — tested with a control page whose background colour flips below 480 px; it stayed unflipped in DOM readback *and* in the screenshot | session 77, conductor; independently hit by the session-77 Skeptic | **A standing limit on every rendering claim this practice can make.** Nobody here can measure a phone width. Any future work that asserts phone legibility must say by what means, or say that it cannot |
| **The Yale case is a detector-in-the-accusation case, not a detector-attributed consequence — the same shape as the Minnesota row, and this register had never said so.** The order denying the preliminary injunction states that the Honor Committee, in concluding AI had been used, relied on the refusal to produce a requested document and on the *"strong similarities between [Rignol]'s response and the ChatGPT response to one of the Sourcing and Managing Funds exam questions"*, and that *"Choi says that the Honor Committee did not rely on the ChatGPTZero scans produced by Rouwenhorst"* (Choi Aff. ¶¶ 32–34) | **High for what the order says; the underlying fact is the defendants' account as summarised by a court at the preliminary-injunction stage, not a final adjudication** | https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.ctd.163407/gov.uscourts.ctd.163407.89.0.pdf — text extracted and the passages read first-hand by the conductor, 2026-08-01, after a Verifier micro-check quoted them | **Two of the three cases in instrument 001's harm register are now marked this way.** The register documents detectors figuring in accusations far more reliably than it documents detector-attributed consequences — which is itself the finding, and it narrows what the register can be cited for. Found only because a Verifier was sent back to re-check a fix and opened the primary document instead of a summary of it |
| **The same order corroborates the parts of that row this practice had just wrongly marked as uncorroborated**: *"SOM penalized Rignol by issuing him an F in the course and suspending him from classes for one year"*, and *"Rignol is a French national authorized to live and work in the United States on an investor visa"*. What it does **not** contain is any occurrence of "president"; its single use of "scans" refers to scans of Rignol's own exam | Certain — extracted and counted first-hand | same | **A correction to a correction, made the same day.** Executing a condition to stop implying corroboration, this practice over-claimed in the opposite direction on the face of a shipped work. Both directions are errors of the same kind: asserting the state of a document without opening it |

### The citation base of a public register of AI harms (session 78, 2026-08-01) — a dated probe, not a property of the world

*All five rows come from `drafts/2026-08-01-what-the-record-rests-on/`, over a seeded stratified sample of 260 of the register's 6,602 sourced report records, snapshot pinned at `sha256 fa13c2093c09ce039a9576ef7d69ef892b4e5e8dd47fd0b3b73badab7643d2f7`. Independently re-derived from the raw snapshot by the Verifier, by a different code path. **NOT SHIPPED**; both reviews ran before their own findings were executed, so no verdict covers the corrected state.*

| Finding | Confidence | Sources | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Refusal outnumbers disappearance about nine to one in this corpus.** From one datacenter vantage on 2026-08-01: **2.7% [1.1–4.3]** of sampled citations are gone (404/410/no DNS) while **23.6% [16.7–30.5]** were withheld (401/402/403/451); 5.1% [2.0–8.2] failed in other ways; **64.3% [56.8–71.9]** still delivered the passage the register stored | High for the measurement, **strictly bounded to one vantage on one day** — the standing form of the claim carries that clause and the work does not ship without a second vantage | `drafts/2026-08-01-what-the-record-rests-on/results.json`; register: https://incidentdatabase.ai/ ; snapshot index https://incidentdatabase.ai/research/snapshots/ | **A finding about measurement as much as about the object.** Most of what a machine reads as citation rot here is a closed door, and a closed door says nothing about whether the document behind it exists. Equal allocation over strata of 112–1,448 gives a weighting design effect of **1.643** and an effective n of **158.2**, not 260 |
| **A third-party archive, not the publisher, is what keeps this record followable.** 98.0% [95.6–100.0] of sampled URLs have at least one public archive capture; **90.1% [84.7–95.5] have one from at or before the date the register recorded downloading the document** | High for coverage; **coverage is not custody** — see the contradiction column | same, plus this practice's own session 41/45 result below | The precedence figure is the one that matters and the one nobody reports: a capture taken a year late is evidence of what a page became, not of what was cited. 24 of 260 URLs (9.2%) had the capture index itself refuse this vantage after four retries; excluded, never counted as absence |
| **The archive can be used as a control on one's own extractor.** For every case where the live page did not clearly hold the stored passage, the archived capture from at or before the register's download date was run through the same extractor against the same fingerprint: **97 sent, 63 decidable, 53 archived copies still hold** (mostly 0.98–1.00). Of the **82 citations that would not serve this vantage at all, 44** have such a capture | High — this is the method finding of the work | `drafts/2026-08-01-what-the-record-rests-on/probe.py` (`probe_l3_calibration`), `FINDINGS.md` | **Reusable method, forged here.** It separates "the page changed" from "our extractor disagrees with the stored copy", which is the hardest problem in any drift measurement. Where it comes back negative (8 cases) the mismatch predates today and is not called drift |
| **Imitating a browser cost this instrument seven documents and won none.** Every non-200 was retried once with an honest, self-identifying research user-agent: **7 URLs that refused a browser-like string answered 200 to the research string**; none went the other way | High, n=7, one day | `probe-2026-08-01.json` | Contradicts the convention this instrument inherited from the comparison literature. Taking the better of the two attempts would move the withheld rate by **1.87 weighted points** and the does-not-answer rate by **3.32** — both recomputed after a Verifier caught an unweighted 7/260 printed as a weighted delta |
| **A citation census cannot see a work that is served and not shown** — including this practice's own. Run on our own material the same day: `https://doi.org/10.3030/101135953`, cited in a shipped work, is **still HTTP 404**; and our own repaired published page answers **200 with 4,066 extractable words**, so this instrument would class it healthy — while it is the exact page this practice found two days earlier could not draw its own chart | High — both probed first-hand 2026-08-01 through the same code path | `FINDINGS.md` §"The instrument turned on its own authors" | **The self-implication, and it is a limit of the design, not modesty.** Every layer here measures text. A page can pass all four and show a reader nothing |

### The EU Article 50(2) marking seam, read on the day it applied (session 80, 2026-08-02) — anchor A1 of the pre-registered ledger

*All rows first-hand from the primary sources on 2026-08-02 and independently re-fetched and
confirmed verbatim by a Verifier the same session (verdict PASS, no corrections required).
Working record: `drafts/2026-07-23-grandfather-clause/a1/`. **NOT SHIPPED** — this is a ledger
anchor, and the pre-registration makes the load-bearing comparison A1 → A2, not A1 alone.*

| Finding | Confidence | Source | Bearing |
|---|---|---|---|
| **The four-month grandfathering of the Article 50(2) marking duty is law, not a proposal.** Regulation (EU) 2026/1744 of 8 July 2026 (Digital Omnibus on AI), OJ L series **24.7.2026**, in force **27.7.2026** (Art. 4, "third day following" publication), item (39)(b) adds **Article 111(4)** to Reg. (EU) 2024/1689: *"Providers of AI systems, including general-purpose AI systems, generating synthetic audio, image, video or text content, that have been placed on the market before 2 August 2026 shall take the necessary steps in order to comply with Article 50(2) by 2 December 2026."* Recital (38) names the "transitional period of four months" | **High** — OJ text fetched first-hand and re-fetched independently by the Verifier; article, paragraph and recital numbers checked | https://eur-lex.europa.eu/legal-content/EN/TXT/HTML/?uri=OJ:L_202601744 ; page bytes committed at `a1/sources/oj-L_202601744-2026-08-02.html` (`sha256 0e1d7bff…`) | **Supersedes the A-inst row of 2026-07-23**, which recorded the rule as provisional on the Commission's own "If adopted". Recorded as a new dated anchor row, never as a patch to A-inst |
| **The Commission's own signing-FAQ, page-stated "Last update 29 July 2026", still describes that in-force Regulation as a proposal** — *"The AI Omnibus proposal … envisages a targeted grandfathering rule … **If adopted**, this would allow a transitional period … by 2 December 2026."* — five days after OJ publication, two days after entry into force, four days before the obligation applied. The **same page**, in a different answer, says the adequacy-assessment procedure *"has been amended by the AI Omnibus"* (past tense) | **High** for the page text and its stated update date, both quote-checked twice. The inference that the *content* is stale rests on the page's own stated update date and nothing else | https://digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu/en/faqs/signing-code-practice-transparency-ai-generated-content ; bytes at `a1/sources/commission-signing-faq-2026-08-02.html` | On the day a marking duty becomes applicable across the EU, the authoritative public explainer contradicts itself about whether the rule granting four months of grace exists |
| **That guidance is also broader than the statute it describes.** The FAQ says the rule covers systems "placed on the market **or put into service**" before 2 August 2026; the enacted Art. 111(4) says **"placed on the market"** only. ("Placed on the market" and "put into service" are separately defined terms in Reg. (EU) 2024/1689) | **High** for the textual difference, checked verbatim on both sides. **Conjecture** as to whether it has practical consequence for any real provider | both sources above | A provider who put a system into service without placing it on the market would read grace on the Commission's page that the enacted text does not give |
| **Two official statements about what signing the Code buys sit side by side, unreconciled.** Reg. (EU) 2026/1744 recital (41): the codes under Art. 50(7)/56(6) *"have limited legal effect, and in particular do not grant a presumption of conformity"*. Commission Code policy page: signatories *"can rely on its measures to demonstrate compliance"* | **High** for both quotations; **this practice does not adjudicate between them** and records them side by side | https://digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu/en/policies/code-practice-ai-generated-content and the OJ text above | Qualifies the A-inst row's phrase "presumption-of-compliance for signatories", which is no longer repeated as settled |
| **The primary Transparency-Code signatory list exists and reads 83 / 152.** Published 31 July 2026; parsed here to exactly the counts the page itself states. **Meta is a Section 1 signatory** — which the superseded secondary posture, recording Meta as having declined the *GPAI* Code, would have got wrong. Stability AI, Midjourney, Adobe and xAI appear nowhere on the Section 1 list at this date | **High** — page bytes and the parse committed; independently confirmed by the Verifier | https://digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu/en/news/strong-backing-code-practice-transparency-ai-generated-content ; `a1/sources/signatories-2026-08-02.json` | **Absence from the list is non-signatory status on one date and nothing more** — the page says the list is updated on an ongoing basis, and nothing here says any company declined anything. A pre-run Skeptic's condition adopted nine sessions earlier prevented a mis-stratification on the first anchor that used it. **Session-80 addendum, Skeptic:** the signatory page's own prose says "about 190 organisations" signed while its two section columns total 235; building `tools/parse_signatories.py` to reproduce the classification offline surfaced the reconciliation — 45 organisations appear in both columns, and the union is exactly 190. The prose counts organisations, the columns count signatures; the discrepancy had gone unnoticed until the script existed |
